**1939 Dec. 14** China reelected to the League Council by League Assembly. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Jan. 1** The National Government ordered national adoption of new hsien (county) system. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Jan. 21** Tao Hsi-Sheng and Kao Tsung-wu revealed Wang Ching-wei's secret treaty with Japanese signed in Shanghai on December 30, 1939. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Jan. 23** Generalissimo Chiang denounced "Japan-Wang secret treaty." — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940** National Government ratified Sino-Soviet Trade Agreement signed in June, 1939. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Feb. 22** The 14th Reincarnation of the Dalai Lama enthorned at Lhasa. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Mar. 1** Ministry of Finance issued first instalment of CNC$1,200,000,000 of the 1940 War Supplies Loan. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Mar. 30** Wang Ching-wei's puppet regime established at Nanking. The Old "Provisional Government" at Peiping and "Reformed Government" at Nanking superseded by Wang's regime. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940** Ministry of Foreign Affairs declared Nanking puppet organization illegal and its acts null and void. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940** American Secretary of State, Cordell Hull, declared United States would not recognize Nanking puppet regime. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Apr. 1** Fifth session of People's Political Council opened in Chungking, closed April 10. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Apr. 20** The US$20-million Sino-American tin loan concluded. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 May 1** The Ministry of Finance issued first instalment of £10-million and US$50-million 1940 reconstruction gold bonds. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 June 12** Ichang fell. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 June 19** Anglo-Japanese agreement reached in Tokyo on Tientsin issue. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 June 20** Japanese blockade of British and French concessions in Tientsin lifted after 372 days of enforcement. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 July 1** Seventh Plenary Session of Kuomintang CEC opened in Chungking, closed July 8. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 July 18** Britain closed Burma Road for three months. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 July 27** President Roosevelt ordered embargo on oil and scrap iron shipments to Japan. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 July 30** Executive Yuan decided to establish National Food Administration. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Aug. 19-20** Japanese bombers raided Chungking. The two days of bombing left four-fifths of Chungking in ruins. — p. 106 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Aug. 27** Last contingent of British troops left Shanghai. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Sept. 1** Second instalment of the CNC$1,200-million 1940 war supplies loan was issued. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Sept. 22** Japan signed agreement with Indo-China. Japanese troops entered Indo-China in three columns. Chinese Ministry of Foreign Affairs lodged protest with France. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Sept. 26** National Government ordered postponement of National Assembly scheduled for November. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Sept. 27** Japan, Germany and Italy signed Tripartite Pact in Berlin. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Oct. 1** Chungking officially made auxiliary capital of China. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Oct. 18** Burma Road reopened after three months closure. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Oct. 29** Japanese troops evacuated Nanning and southern Kwangsi. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Nov. 1** Second instalment of the £10-million and US$50,000,000 1940 reconstruction gold loan was issued. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Nov. 30** Wang Ching-wei puppet regime concluded "treaty" with Japan giving her virtual control over lower Yangtze, north China and Inner Mongolia. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1940 Dec. 1** National Government declared treaty signed between Wang Ching-wei and Japanese illegal. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Jan. 18** National Military Council ordered disbandment of New Fourth Army as measure of military discipline. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Feb. 1** The first instalment of CNC$1,200-million 1941 war supplies loan issued. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Feb. 4** The US$50-million Sino-American metal loan concluded. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Mar. 1** First Session of the Second People's Political Council opened in Chungking; closed March 10. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941** First instalment of CNC$1,200-million 1941 reconstruction loan issued. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Mar. 24** Eighth Plenary Session of Kuomintang CEC opened in Chungking; closed April 2. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Mar. 28** Battle of Shangkao in northern Kiangsi ended. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Apr. 14** Referring to Soviet-Japanese Neutrality Pact, signed April 13 in Moscow, Foreign Minister Wang Chung-hui declared Outer Mongolia and Northeastern Provinces Chinese territory and the Soviet-Japanese declaration not binding on China. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Apr. 15** President Roosevelt announced list of materials for China under Lend-Lease Act. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Apr. 21** Japanese entered Foochow. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Apr. 25** In Washington, the United States and Great Britain signed separate but parallel agreements with China for stabilization of Chinese national currency. The Sino-American agreement provided for American stabilization fund of $US50-million, while Sino-British agreement provided for a British stabilization fund of £5-million. Chinese banks would raise another US$20-million for same purpose. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 May 13** Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced exchange of diplomatic representatives with Australia. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 June 1** Second instalment of CNC$1,200-million 1941 war supplies loan issued. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 June 5** Third Sino-British export credit loan £5-million concluded. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 June 17** Ministry of Food established under Executive Yuan. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 June 18** Sino-British Yunnan-Burma boundary demarcation agreement signed in Chungking. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 July 1** Second instalment of CNC$1-200-million 1941 reconstruction loan issued. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941** Germany, Italy and Rumania recognized Nanking puppet regime. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 July 2** China severed diplomatic relations with Germany and Italy, and on July 10 with Rumania. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 July 20** China severed diplomatic relations with Denmark. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 July 25** Vichy revealed that Indo-China authorities agreed to "Japanese protection of peace" in entire Indo-China. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941** Great Britain cancelled Anglo-Japanese Treaty of Commerce and Navigation and ordered freezing of Chinese and Japanese assets in British Empire. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941** United States ordered freezing of Chinese and Japanese assets in America. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 July 28** Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced China considered entire Indo-China under Japanese military occupation. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941** Dutch East Indies ordered freezing of Japanese assets, cancelled Dutch-Japanese financial agreement and ordered embargo of Dutch oil to Japan. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Aug. 1** Thailand recognized puppet regime in Northeastern Provinces and joined Japanese "Sphere of Co-Prosperity in East Asia." — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941** United States ordered oil embargo on Japan. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Aug. 14** President Roosevelt and Prime Minister Churchill issued joint declaration of "Atlantic Charter." — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Aug. 17** China declared intention to abide by Atlantic Charter. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Aug. 26** China recognized Czech Government under President Benes. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Aug. 28** Chinese Goodwill Mission left Chungking for Burma; returned September 8. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Aug. 29** Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced exchange of diplomatic representatives between China and Canada. — p. 107 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Sept. 3** Chinese recaptured Foochow. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Oct. 1** Third instalment of CNC$1,200-million 1941 war supplies loan issued. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Oct. 4** Chengchow fell. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Oct. 8** Chinese won second battle of Changsha. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Oct. 9** American Military Mission under Maj.-Gen. John Magruder arrived in Chungking. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Oct. 31** Chinese recaptured Chengchow. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Nov. 17** Second Session of Second People's Political Council opened in Chungking for nine days. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Nov. 28** Last group of American marines evacuated from Shanghai. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Dec. 8 (U.S. Dec. 7)** Japan declared war on United States and Great Britain, after attacking Pearl Harbor, Hongkong and other American and British bases in Far East. Shanghai, Kulangsu and Tientsin Settlements and concessions occupied by the Japanese. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941** Great Britain declared war on Japan. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Dec. 9 (U.S. Dec. 8)** China declared war on Japan, Germany and Italy. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941** United States declared war on Japan. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Dec. 15** Ninth Plenary Session of Kuomintang CEC opened in Chungking until December 23. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Dec. 17** London announced formation of British Military Mission to China. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Dec. 20** The American Volunteer Group of the Chinese Air Force took part in active combat over Kunming. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Dec. 22** Gen. Sir Archibald Wavell, commander-in-chief of India, and Maj.-Gen. Brett of the American Army Air Corps, arrived in Chungking for two days consultation with Generalissimo Chiang. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1941 Dec. 23** T. V. Soong appointed Minister of Foreign Affairs. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Jan. 1** Joint declaration of the United Nations signed in Washington. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Jan. 2** Chinese troops entered Burma. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Jan. 3** Generalissimo Chiang accepted command of the China Theater of war. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Jan. 15** Third Battle of Changsha won by Chinese. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Feb. 1** The 1942 U. S. Government Credit to China, US$500-million, concluded. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Feb. 4** Generalissimo and Madame Chiang Kai-shek arrived in India, departed Feb. 22. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Feb. 25** Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced resumption of diplomatic relations between China and Poland after latter withdrew recognition of puppet regime in Chinese Northeastern Provinces. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Mar. 3** Generalissimo Chiang arrived in Lashio for military conference with General Wavell. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Mar. 4** Gen. Joseph Stilwell arrived in Chungking to assume command of all American Armed forces in China, Burma and India. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Mar. 5** Generalissimo and Madame Chiang returned to Chungking from visit to India and Burma. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Mar. 10** Gen. Joseph Stilwell appointed chief of staff of the China Theater. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942** Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced China and India would exchange resident representatives. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Mar. 12** Chinese Military Mission headed by Gen. Hsiung Shih-hui, left Chungking for America, arrived April 13. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Mar. 29** National Mobilization Act promulgated. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Mar. 30** President Roosevelt announced establishment of Pacific War Council in Washington, comprising China, United States, Great Britain, Australia, New Zealand, Canada and Netherlands. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Apr. 24** Generalissimo Chiang received British decoration of Grand Cross of the Bath awarded by King George VI. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Apr. 29** Chinese troops evacuated Lashio in northeastern Burma. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 May 1** The 1942 Allied Victory Gold Loan, US$100-million, floated. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942** Chinese troops evacuated Mandalay in central Burma. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 May 3** Japanese troops advanced into western Yunnan along the Burma Road. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 May 5** National Mobilization Act went into effect. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 May 9** Japanese invading army stopped on the Salween River by Chinese. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 May 11** Tengchung fell. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 May 29** Kinhwa fell. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 June 2** Foreign Minister T. V. Soong and American Secretary of State Cordell Hull signed Sino-American Lend-Lease agreement in Washington. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 July 1** The 1942 Allied Victory Loan of CNC$1-billion issued. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 July 4** American Volunteer Group of Chinese Air Force superseded by the China Task Force of U. S. Army Air Force. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Aug. 28** Chinese troops recaptured major part of Chekiang-Kiangsi railway. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Sept. 21** Generalissimo Chiang concluded month-long trip to northwest. He visited Kansu, Ningsia and Chinghai. Madame Chiang visited Sinkiang. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Sept. 29** Wendell Willkie arrived in Sinkiang from U.S.S.R. He arrived in Chungking October 2 and departed October 9. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Oct. 10** American and British governments announced intention of relinquishing extraterritorial and other related rights in China. — p. 108 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Oct. 22** First session of Third People's Political Council convened; adjourned October 31. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Nov. 1** Japan established Ministry of Great East Asia to rule occupied territories. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Nov. 10** British Parliamentary Mission arrived in Chungking, departed December 11. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Nov. 12** Tenth Plenary Session of Kuomintang CEC opened in Chungking; closed November 27. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1942 Nov. 27** Madame Chiang arrived in New York for medical treatment. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Jan. 5** The Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced Chinese participation in United Nations declaration signed in London. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Jan. 11** New Sino-American and Sino-British treaties concluded. Sino-American treaty signed in Washington, Sino-British treaty in Chungking. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Feb. 5** Gen. H. H. Arnold, chief of the United States Army Air Force, and Sir John Dill, chief of staff, representing President Roosevelt and Prime Minister Churchill respectively, arrived in Chungking for two-day conference with Generalissimo Chiang. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Feb. 17** Madame Chiang arrived in Washington as guest of President and Mrs. Roosevelt at White House. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943** Japanese troops landed at Kwangchowwan. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Feb. 18** Madame Chiang addressed combined session of U. S. Senate and House of Representatives. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Feb. 19** American Lend-Lease Administration announced establishment of China Bureau. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Feb. 24** Chinese Government lodged protest with Vichy regime over Japanese landing at Kwangchowwan. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Feb. 26** Generalissimo Chiang broadcast to people of Thailand. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Mar. 6** The 14th Air Force of United States Army began operation in China. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Mar. 15** Generalissimo Chiang visited Kweichow. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Apr. 4** Madame Chiang ended official visit to U. S. and returned to New York after visiting Chicago, San Francisco and Los Angeles. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Apr. 22** Foreign Minister T. V. Soong visited Canada. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 May 19** Ministry of Foreign Affairs lodged protest with Vichy regime over transfer of French Concession and other rights to Nanking puppets. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 May 20** Ratification of new Sino-American and Sino-British treaties exchanged in Washington and Chungking, respectively. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 May 21** Chinese and American governments exchanged notes in Chungking concerning criminal jurisdiction over American armed forces in China. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 June 1** The 1943 Allied Victory Loan, CNC$3-billion, issued. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 June 10** Washington announced agreement reached among China, the United States, Great Britain and U.S.S.R. on Inter-Allied Relief and Rehabilitation Administration. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 June 14** Battle of western Hupeh ended. Japanese retreated to original positions. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 June 15** Madame Chiang arrived in Canada. Addressed joint session of Canadian Parliament July 16. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 June 27** Madame Chiang began journey home. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 June 28** Generalissimo Chiang received Grand Cordon of the Aztec Eagle from Mexican Government. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 July 7** Generalissimo Chiang received Legion of Merit, degree of chief commander, from the U. S. government. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Aug. 1** Lin Sen, chairman of National Government, died. Generalissimo Chiang appointed acting chairman. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943** Chinese Government severed diplomatic relations with Vichy regime. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Aug. 22** Foreign Minister T. V. Soong in Quebec to join Roosevelt-Churchill conference. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Aug. 27** China recognized French Committee for National Liberation in London. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Sept. 6** The 11th Plenary Session of Kuomintang CEC opened in Chungking; closed September 13. Generalissimo Chiang elected chairman of National Government. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Sept. 18** Second Session of Third People's Political Council convened in Chungking; closed September 27. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Oct. 10** Generalissimo Chiang assumed office as chairman of National Government. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Oct. 16** Lord Mountbatten, supreme commander in Southeast Asia, arrived in Chungking for military conference with Chinese military leaders. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Oct. 30** Declaration on general security (Moscow Declaration) signed in Moscow by China, United States, Great Britain and U.S.S.R. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Nov. 4** Chinese-American Composite Wing of Chinese Air Force went into action. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Nov. 9** United Nations Relief and Rehabilitation agreement was signed in Washington. — p. 109 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Nov. 18** Goodwill Mission to Great Britain left Chungking for India en route to England. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Nov. 21** Generalissimo Chiang, Madame Chiang, arrived in Cairo for conference with President Roosevelt and Prime Minister Churchill. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Dec. 1** Joint declaration of Cairo Conference issued. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943** Generalissimo and Madame Chiang returned to Chungking from Cairo. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Dec. 3** Japanese troops entered Changteh. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Dec. 9** Changteh recaptured. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Dec. 17** Lieut.-Gen. A. Carton de Wiart, Prime Minister Churchill's personal representative to Generalissimo Chiang, arrived in Chungking. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1943 Dec. 29** Chinese Goodwill Mission to Great Britain received by King George VI. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Jan. 25** British Prime Minister Churchill received Chinese Goodwill Mission. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Feb. 7** Chinese Military Mission, headed by General Yang Chieh, arrived in London. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Mar. 5** Chinese troops recaptured Maingkwan in northern Burma. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Mar. 27** Goodwill Mission to Great Britain returned to Chungking. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Apr. 14** New Sino-Canadian treaty signed in Ottawa. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Apr. 22** Joint Statements by experts on the establishment of an international monetary fund of the United and Associated Nations published in Washington, London, and Chungking. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944** Chengchow fell. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Apr. 23** China elected to governing body of the International Labor Office. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 May 1** Gen. Z. Pechkoff, representative of French National Liberation Committee, presented credentials to President Chiang Kai-shek. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 May 2** Agreement on fourth Sino-British export credit loan of £50,000,000 signed in London. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944** Sino-British Lend-Lease agreement signed in London. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 May 11** Chinese forces in western Yunnan established bridgeheads west of Salween river. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 May 17** Chinese and American forces began siege of Myitkyina in northern Burma. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944** Military Mission headed by Gen. Shang Chen arrived in Washington. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 May 20** The 12th Plenary Session of the Kuomintang Fifth CEC opened in Chungking until May 26. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 May 25** Loyang fell. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 June 15** American superfortresses raided Japan proper for the first time. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 June 16** Kamaing in northern Burma captured by Chinese. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 June 17** Japanese completed occupation of Peiping-Hankow railway. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 June 18** Changsha fell. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 June 21** Henry Wallace arrived in Chungking. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 June 23** Dr. H. H. Kung, vice-president of Executive Yuan, arrived in Washington for United Nations monetary and finance conference. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 June 25** Mogaung, in northern Burma, captured by Chinese. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 June 26** President Roosevelt presented scroll to the men, women, and children of Chungking for bravery under enemy bombing. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 July 1** The 1944 Allied Victory Loan of CNC$5-billion floated. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 July 2** Henry Wallace left for America. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 July 9** Madame Chiang left for America for medical treatment. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Aug. 1** Regulations for safeguarding the freedom of person were enforced. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Aug. 3** Organized Japanese resistance at Myitkyina ceased. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Aug. 8** Hengyang fell. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Aug. 28** V. K. Wellington Koo, head of the Chinese Delegation to the Dumbarton Oaks Conference, arrived in Washington. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Sept. 5** Third session of Third People's Political Council opened in Chungking until September 18. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Sept. 6** President Roosevelt's personal emissaries, Donald Nelson and Maj.-Gen. Patrick Hurley, arrived in Chungking. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944** Special units from Chinese Expeditionary Force in western Yunnan and Chinese Army in India fighting in northern Burma joined forces at Kaoliangkung pass on Yunnan-Burma border, marking first overland contact between China and Southeast Asia Theater since Japanese occupation of Burma in 1942. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Sept. 14** Chinese troops recaptured Tengchung city. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Sept. 15** Gen. Chang Chih-chung, government representative, and Lin Tsu-han, communist representative, reported on the Government-Communist negotiations at People's Political Council. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Sept. 28** French Government presented the Yangtze river gunboat S.S. Le Balny to Chinese Government. The gunboat was renamed Fa Ku. — p. 110 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Sept. 29** Chinese-American-British phases of Dumbarton Oaks Conference began. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Oct. 5** Foochow fell. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Oct. 9** Results of Dumbarton Oaks Conference published. Establishment of general assembly, security council and international court of justice proposed. Chinese-American-British phase of the Conference concluded. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Oct. 14** Government called 100,000 educated youth to volunteer for military service. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Oct. 23** China, together with United States, Great Britain, and U.S.S.R., announced recognition of Provisional Government of France. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Oct. 26** The Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced recognition of Government of Italy. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Oct. 29** The White House announced recall (October 24) of Gen. Joseph Stilwell. Maj.-Gen. Albert C. Wedemeyer appointed Commanding General of U. S. Forces in China Theater. He was also appointed chief of staff to Generalissimo Chiang, supreme commander of China Theater. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944** Lieut.-Gen. Daniel I. Sultan was appointed Commanding General of U. S. Army Forces in Burma and India Theater. He was appointed by Generalissimo Chiang as commander of all Chinese forces based in India. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Nov. 3** Chinese troops recaptured Lungling. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Nov. 10** Kweilin fell. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944** Wang Ching-wei died in Japan. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Nov. 11** Liuchow fell. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Nov. 15** Ishan fell. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Nov. 16** Chinese War Production Board established under the direction of Wong Wen-hao. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944** The Ministry of Conscription organized with Gen. Lu Chung-lin as minister. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944** Donald Nelson returned to Chungking with American experts to assist Chinese War Production Board. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Nov. 19** Lieut.-Gen. D. I. Sultan announced construction of oil pipeline from India to China. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944** Supreme National Defense Council appointed Gen. Chen Cheng, Minister of War; Chang Li-sheng, Minister of Interior; O. K. Yui, Minister of Finance; and Chu Chia-hua, Minister of Education. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Nov. 21** Chinese Training and Combat Command of the U. S. Forces, China Theater, was established with Brig.-Gen. Frank Dorn as commanding general. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Nov. 29** United National War Crimes Sub-Commission for the Far East and Pacific formed in Chungking with Wang Chung-hui as chairman. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Dec. 1** Chinese troops recaptured Chefang in western Yunnan. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Dec. 4** T. V. Soong appointed acting president of Executive Yuan. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Dec. 5** Tushan fell and recaptured on December 8. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Dec. 15** Chinese troops recaptured Bhamo in northern Burma. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1944 Dec. 25** Headquarters of Chinese Ground Forces inaugurated in Kunming with Gen. Ho Ying-chin as commander-in-chief. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Jan. 1** President Chiang Kai-shek promised to call National Assembly to adopt constitution before end of the war. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** War Transport Board was formed under the direction of Gen. Yu Fei-peng. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Jan. 8** Maj.-Gen. Patrick Hurley, U. S. Ambassador to China, presented credentials to President Chiang Kai-shek. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Jan. 15** Chinese troops recaptured Namkhan in northern Burma. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Jan. 20** Chinese troops recaptured Wanting on Yunnan-Burma border. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Jan. 21** Test convoy over the Tengchung cutoff (from Myitkyina to Kunming) arrived in Kunming. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Jan. 22** Lieut-Gen. D. I. Sultan announced that Ledo-Burma Road was entirely cleared of Japs. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Jan. 23** Chinese National Relief and Rehabilitation Administration formally inaugurated with T. F. Tsiang as director. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Jan. 28** President Chiang broadcast to American and British peoples naming Ledo-Burma road the Stilwell Road in honor of Gen. Joseph Stilwell. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** First convoy from India passed Wanting entering Chinese territory. The convoy welcomed by official Chinese delegation headed by T. V. Soong. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Feb. 4** First convoy over Stilwell Road arrived in Kunming. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Feb. 12** First regular convoy over Tengchung cutoff arrived in Kunming. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 1** President Chiang announced National Assembly would convene November 12 to adopt constitution. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 5** Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced joint invitations to United Nations Conference on International Organization at San Francisco issued by China, U.S.S.R., United Kingdom and United States. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 7** Lord and Lady Mountbatten arrived in Chungking. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Chinese troops recaptured Lashio in northern Burma. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 9** Japanese troops in Indo-China disarmed all French and Indo-Chinese troops. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 13** Japanese troops seized Kwangchowwan. — p. 111 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 16** Hsipaw, west of Lashio, captured by Chinese. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 20** Mandalay taken by British. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 26** Chinese delegation to San Francisco Conference named. T. V. Soong appointed head. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 28** Wilbur Forrest, Ralph McGill, and Carl Ackerman, representatives of American Association of Newspaper Editors, arrived in Chungking on world tour. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Mar. 29** Siangyang in northwestern Hupeh fell. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Apr. 1** Japanese Government announced incorporation of Korea and Formosa into Japan Proper. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Apr. 3** Ratification of Sino-Canadian Treaty, signed on April 17, 1944, exchanged in Chungking. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Apr. 4** Nanyang in southwest Honan captured by Japanese . — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Apr. 5** U.S.S.R. renounced Soviet-Japanese Neutrality Pact. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Apr. 11** Laohokow in northwestern Hupeh fell. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Apr. 12** President Roosevelt died. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Laohokow recaptured by Chinese. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Apr. 16** Chinese regained Siangyang. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Apr. 22** U. S. Ambassador Patrick Hurley returned to Chungking from America via London and Moscow. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Apr. 25** United Nations Conference on International Organization held at San Francisco. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 5** Kuomintang Sixth National Conference opened in Chungking until May 18. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 8** German unconditional surrender officially announced. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 17** President Chiang unanimously reelected Tsungtsai of Kuomintang. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 18** Chinese recaptured Foochow. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 20** Hochih in northern Kwangsi recovered. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** India-China oil pipeline completed. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 21** Chingchengkiang in Kwangsi recaptured by Chinese. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 27** Chinese recaptured Nanning. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 28** First Plenary Session of Sixth Kuomintang CEC opened in Chungking until May 31. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 30** The Ministry of Foreign Affairs announced establishment of diplomatic relations with Argentina. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 May 31** Kuomintang CEC elected T. V. Soong president and Wong Wen-hao, vice president of Executive Yuan. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 June 6** Chinese crossed border from Kwangsi into Indo-China. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 June 8** Chinese recaptured Lungchow in southwestern Kwangsi. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 June 11** For National Government, H. H. Kung conferred Special Grand Cordon of Pao-ting (Precious Tripod) upon Admirals of the Fleet William Leahy and Ernest King, and General of the Army Douglas MacArthur. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 June 14** Chinese recaptured Ishan in northern Kwangsi. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 June 18** Chinese regained Wenchow in Chekiang. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 June 26** United Nations Charter signed in San Francisco. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 June 27** T. V. Soong left for Moscow. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Chinese troops recaptured Liuchow in Kwangsi. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 June 30** T. V. Soong and party arrived in Moscow and received by Marshal Stalin. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 1** Six People's Political Councillors left Chungking for Yenan to consult with Mao Tse-tung. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 4** T. V. Soong received by Kalinin. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 7** First session of Fourth People's Political Council opened in Chungking. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Sino-British agreement concerning jurisdiction of one nation's armed forces in the territory of the other was signed in Chungking. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 11** Maj.-Gen. Claire L. Chennault, retired as commander of the 14th U.S.A.A.F. in Kunming. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 17** T. V. Soong arrived in Chungking from Moscow with Soviet Ambassador A. A. Petrov. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 18** Big Three Conference of President Truman, Prime Minister Churchill, and Generalissimo Stalin, opened at Potsdam, Germany. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 25** O. K. Yui appointed governor of Central Bank of China to succeed H. H. Kung. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 26** President Chiang, President Truman, and Prime Minister Churchill issued ultimatum to Japan, ordering her to surrender unconditionally or to face prompt and utter destruction. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 27** Chinese troops recaptured Kweilin in Kwangsi. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 July 30** Wang Shih-chieh was appointed minister of foreign affairs to succeed T. V. Soong. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 2** Big Three Conference at Potsdam ended. A joint communique published simultaneously in Washington, London and Moscow the next day. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 5** T. V. Soong and Wang Shih-chieh left Chungking for Moscow to continue Sino-Soviet negotiations. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 6** American superfortress dropped first atomic bomb on Hiroshima. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 7** T. V. Soong and Wang Shih-chieh arrived in Moscow. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 8** U.S.S.R. declared war on Japan as of August 9. — p. 112 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 9** Soviet army entered Manchuria. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Second Atomic bomb was dropped on Nagasaki. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 10** Radio Tokyo announced that Japan was ready to accept the Potsdam Declaration. Official statement of Japanese Government made through the Swiss Government. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 14** Japan accepted Allied demand for unconditional surrender, the Japanese Emperor to rule country subject to authority of Supreme Allied Commander. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Sino-Soviet Treaty of Friendship and Alliance signed in Moscow. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 15** Legislative Yuan unanimously approved the United Nations Charter. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** President Chiang invited Mao Tse-tung to come to Chungking for conference. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 16** T. V. Soong arrived in Washington for conference with President Truman. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 17** President Chiang sent a second telegram to Mao Tse-tung inviting him to Chungking for conference. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 20** Wang Shih-chieh returned from Moscow. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 21** Maj.-Gen. Takeo Imai, representing Gen. Okamura, arrived in Chihkiang to receive surrender conditions from Lieut.-Gen. Hsiao Yi-shu, chief of staff to Gen. Ho Ying-chin. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** American Lend-Lease services were terminated. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 23** President Chiang sent third telegram to Mao Tse-tung inviting him to Chungking for conference. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 24** President Chiang in a statement clarified China's stand in relation to principle of nationalism and world peace. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Legislative Yuan ratified the Sino-Soviet Treaty of Friendship and Alliance. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** President Chiang signed the United Nations Charter on behalf of China. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 26** Sino-Soviet Treaty of Friendship and Alliance published. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 27** Lieut.-Gen. Leng Hsin, deputy chief of staff to Gen. Ho Ying-chin, arrived in Nanking with 159 officers and men to establish the forward echelon of headquarters of Chinese Ground Forces. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Allied troops began landing in Japan. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 28** Chinese gendarmes entered Shanghai. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Mao Tse-tung arrived in Chungking from Yenan, accompanied by U. S. Ambassador Patrick Hurley. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 29** Gen. Chen Yi appointed governor of Taiwan. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 30** T. V. Soong arrived in Ottawa, Canada, for discussions with Canadian authorities. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Aug. 31** Chinese troops entered Indo-China and liberated Laokai. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Gen. Hsiung Shih-hui appointed director of Generalissimo's headquarters in the Northeastern Provinces. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Three Northeastern Provinces were divided into nine provinces. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 2** Instrument for Japanese surrender signed on board U.S.S. Missouri by Shigemitsu and Gen. Umetsu. Gen. MacArthur signed for the Allies. Gen. Hsu Yung-chang represented China. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 5** First unit of Chinese New Sixth Army arrived in Nanking by air. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945** Wang Shih-chieh left Chungking for London to attend Foreign Ministers Conference. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 6** First unit of Chinese 49th Army arrived in Shanghai by air. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 9** Gen. Ho Ying-chin received formal surrender of Japanese in China from Gen. Okamura in Nanking. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 12** Chinese took over Shanghai and completed reoccupation of Canton. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 14** Premier T. V. Soong concluded series of conversations with President Truman in Washington. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 17** Gen. Ho Ying-chin announced all Japanese civilians in China would be deported to Japan. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 19** Premier T. V. Soong declared that China would respect French sovereignty in Indo-China. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 20** T. F. Tsiang made known China's request for US$945-million worth of UNRRA relief. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Sept. 25** Disarmament of Japanese troops in Nanking completed. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))

**1945 Oct. 3** Gen. Lu Han appointed governor of Yunnan Province to succeed Gen. Lung Yun. — p. 113 ([scan part 1, leaf 60](https://archive.org/details/china-handbook-1950/China%20Handbook%201950%20Part%201%20of%203/page/n60/mode/1up))
